## 2026-10-31 — Canonical receipt origins, resolution-qualified activation and flood publication race checks

**Scope:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck` on `feature/world-engine-v2`. The previously unverified baseline `8e0da524236c7515b504228fd208f3ae45167043` is now **CI PASSED**: https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41157532250 . Its pending-water discovery tests are therefore verified by Maven CI, not merely committed.

**Implemented and verified:**
- The spherical pending-region scheduler and persisted incremental discovery now reject receipts whose **source** is a noncanonical longitude-seam or polar alias, as well as invalid destinations. Previously, two keys with the same geographic source (`x=0` and `x=maxX`) could represent duplicate discharge while both targeting a canonical destination. The new guard runs before provider loading or flood checkpoint mutation. The generic legacy transfer key still accepts some local aliases; the strict spherical entrypoints must be used for spherical routing.
- Added resolution-qualified `HydrologySphericalRegionSchedulerV2.loadWithPending(..., level, divisions, ...)` and `HydrologyWorldRunnerV2.advanceSphericalPendingRegionsAndQueue(..., level, divisions, ...)`. They reject a parked receipt with the same level but different sampling divisions before any provider load or water consumption. The older level-only overload remains for compatibility and **cannot** independently prove the provider's divisions. This is single-resolution safety, **not** mixed-LOD routing.
- `HydrologyIncrementalRegionBridgeV2.partitionPersisted` now reloads and compares the durable flood checkpoint **after** enumerating caller-owned water, rejecting an optimistic publication if another local writer advanced the flood during partitioning. This is not a distributed CAS.
- Added a deterministic integration regression that inserts a new durable world receipt while pending-water flood discovery is sampling terrain. The discovery must abort before saving a stale flood frontier; both receipts remain undelivered and the previous flood checkpoint remains intact.

**New regression coverage:** `HydrologySphericalReceiptOriginV2Test` (duplicate seam-source alias rejected before loading, valid canonical seam accepted, aliased persisted discovery rejected without water loss); `HydrologySphericalResolutionContractV2Test` (same-level wrong divisions rejected, valid divisions accepted, failed world advance preserves epoch and parked water); `HydrologyIncrementalRegionBridgeV2Test` (checkpoint change during owned-water enumeration rejected, concurrent receipt insertion during flood discovery rejected with pending water preserved).

## 2026-10-31 — Opt-in atomic incremental flood/world handoff (verified)

**Scope:** `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, branch `feature/world-engine-v2` only. Prior baseline `b4e2c09e8212eb8a94c14e0604979f607452e3ea` passed the full Java CI workflow: https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41162097125 .

**Implemented and verified:**
- `HydrologyIncrementalAtomicEpochV2` embeds the deterministic priority-flood replay checkpoint (terrain revision, outlet anchors, settled count, settled/frontier SHA-256 digest) inside the existing atomically replaced world+receipt checkpoint. This avoids the two-file commit gap **for this opt-in world payload format**. It does not modify or automatically migrate older world checkpoint formats or the independent flood checkpoint store.
- `advance` independently replays the prior committed flood state and the proposed additional settlements against the supplied immutable terrain oracle before partitioning water. The persisted world identity must not regress or change verified outlets/terrain revision. All due undelivered receipts must have canonical, settled destinations of the same sampling resolution. Exactly one water source is required for each settled vertex.
- Concurrent receipt changes detected during owned-water enumeration abort before epoch advancement. The underlying world store's existing optimistic freshness guard protects against stale runner checkpoint overwrites; it remains **single-writer local filesystem protection**, not cross-process atomic CAS.
- `resumeBound` reconstructs a flood session directly from the authoritative world checkpoint, avoiding independent flood-file recovery for this path.
- New tests cover codec roundtrip and malformed payloads, replay-verified settlement advancement, one-time cross-region receipt delivery with local water, crash/restart recovery, no replay after reopening, unsettled due-water rejection, terrain mismatch, duplicate/missing water, and concurrent incoming receipt rejection.

**Still unfinished:** This is an opt-in local-file handoff, not automatic activation from `discoverPendingPersisted`. The caller must provide a truthful immutable terrain oracle, complete water ownership for settled vertices, and verified outlets. Unsettled terrain cannot contribute its water; an incomplete flood is not a complete global catchment. Existing unbound world states and separate flood files are not migrated. Mixed-resolution routing, atomic terrain/erosion/sediment/lake/snow/soil reconciliation, physically timed flood movement, multi-spillway lakes, groundwater, calibrated climate, distributed CAS, long-horizon planetary benchmarks and receipt-history compaction remain open. **Do not advance to other world layers.**

**Final regression additions:** Future-dated water receipts remain parked until their due epoch. Three bounded incremental flood advancements survive restart without a separate flood file. The opt-in advance path synchronizes on its runner to prevent concurrent same-process receipt writes between preflight and commit; reentrant changes are detected and rejected. A noncanonical longitude-seam origin is rejected without consuming its parked water. All these scenarios passed in the final code/test CI cited above.

The existing `advanceClosedWindow` and `advanceVerifiedFrontier` retain their strict no-pending-receipt behavior.

## 2026-10-31 — Mixed-resolution durable receipts and multi-region continuation

**Implemented:**
- `HydrologyMixedResolutionEpochV2` introduces per-edge, epoch-scoped, physical-coordinate-equivalent receipt keys. Same-volume alias replays are idempotent; conflicting replays and forks from one source to two destinations in the same epoch are rejected. It stages due incoming water only at currently loaded geographic owners, keeps future/unloaded receipts pending, and rejects importing a source that is already represented in the loaded routing graph. Delivery markers and receipts have a deterministic versioned snapshot codec.
- `HydrologyMixedResolutionCheckpointStoreV2` durably stores an opaque world payload, accepted receipts, and consumed markers together in one SHA-256-checked local file using an atomic rename and file/directory sync. It supports durable receipt acceptance before acknowledgment, optimistic stale-writer rejection, restart recovery, and advancing the mixed-LOD solve and consumption together. **Single-writer local filesystem only**, not distributed or multi-process CAS.
- `HydrologyMixedResolutionEpochV2.exportFrontier` emits individual source-edge receipts for known unloaded downstream destinations, rather than collapsing their provenance into the aggregate pending-outflow map. It rejects unresolved sinks, duplicate owners, mismatched graph/result ownership, and unaccounted pending water.
- New JUnit regressions cover mixed-LOD alias replay, conservation, future and unloaded receipts, overflow rollback, checkpoint corruption, stale writes, confluence export and a **three-region, two-unloaded-frontier** route. The final test verifies imported water waits until its due epoch, then reaches the verified mouth without replay.

### 2026-10-31 — Sender receipt chronology audit

## 2026-10-31 — Future-arrival correctness and receiver-side retention reverse audit

**Branch-only commits:** `b78518d4` (fix sender chronology false positive), `2d1e5a81` (future-arrival regression), `f400e0d6` (reverse audit for explicitly supplied receiver destinations), `c0d20de3` (reverse-audit regressions), `fbda3ec4` (reject receipts outside supplied receiver ownership), `abb8a8da` (ownership regression), plus this documentation commit.

**Bug corrected:** A sender may atomically queue water in an earlier committed epoch for a *future arrival epoch*, using the existing `advanceAndQueue(..., arrivalEpoch)` contract. The prior read-only history audit incorrectly treated `sender.epoch() < receipt.epoch()` as impossible. Removed this false blocker; sender receipt epochs represent destination arrival, not necessarily sender commit time. The previous test that asserted this false rejection was replaced with a valid future-arrival regression.

**Additional retention audit:** `HydrologyMixedResolutionHistoryAuditV2.auditForDestinations` now cross-checks supplied sender outboxes against a caller-supplied physical receiver-destination inventory. It blocks known old sender transfers missing from the receiver, whether acknowledged or still pending; rejects duplicate receiver geographic ownership and receiver receipts outside the declared ownership; ignores transfers to other regions and those beyond the audited arrival horizon. It retains the original read-only `audit` API and does **not** prune, mutate, or authorize deletion of any receipts.

**Tests and verification:** Added five new repository JUnit test methods (missing acknowledged water, old pending water with horizon boundary, unrelated destination, duplicate/empty ownership, receiver ownership mismatch) and replaced one incorrect sender-chronology test. A standalone Java 21 / `javac --release 17` smoke harness compiled equivalent audit logic with minimal model stubs and passed six scenarios: valid future arrival, missing acknowledged water, horizon exclusion, ownership mismatch, matched receipt, duplicate ownership. This is **not** an execution of the exact committed JUnit suite or the full Maven build. The last independently checked full workflow is the earlier baseline https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41236481770 (success). The GitHub connector's commit-workflow lookup is PR-filtered and returned no push runs for new HEAD `abb8a8da`; combined statuses were also empty. **New-commit CI status remains unverified**, not assumed passing.

**Outstanding / acceptance gaps:** This remains a caller-supplied, read-only **local evidence** audit. It cannot prove that the sender inventory is complete, authenticate source ownership, establish a durable global replay watermark, prevent delayed remote retries, reconstruct a lost receiver checkpoint, or make history compaction safe. No pruning is enabled. Mixed-resolution cross-process dispatch is not globally atomic. Automatic planetary drainage discovery, real erosion-induced in-flight route migration, persistent lake/terrain/sediment coupling, multiple spillways, groundwater, calibrated seasonal forcing, timed flooding, long-run planetary endurance, and production save integration remain unfinished. **Hydrology is NOT complete.**

**Next:** Verify the new push-triggered CI in GitHub Actions and fix any failures. Establish a durable sender-inventory/replay barrier before implementing compaction. Then continue actual route-migration integration and lake/sediment coupling. No other branches, PRs, production resources, or paid infrastructure were changed.

## 2026-11-01 — Lake sediment infill and multi-season water/sediment conservation

**Scope:** `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, `feature/world-engine-v2` only. Started from `42fa19bdaf911c7d9292fe300b2ee606d99e0270`. Checked current-head GitHub workflow lookup and combined statuses before implementing changes: both were empty; the available workflow lookup filters PR-triggered runs and cannot establish push-triggered CI success. Independently rechecked the earlier baseline workflow https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41236481770: its test job and listed test/world/browser steps concluded success. **No full CI result for this run's commits has been independently verified.**

**Implemented:**
- `5c0bca8eefc6172097d1688643e1ab85aadf495b`: `LakeSedimentEvolutionV2.deposit` converts caller-supplied sediment mass and dry bulk density to volume, fills lowest lake-bed columns first across a fixed known spill level, recomputes storage capacity, and explicitly exports water displaced by sediment. Excess sediment is separately exported. Complete silting removes reservoir storage rather than creating a zero-capacity `Basin` that the existing geometry contract forbids. The original basin remains unchanged.
- `4a061fcc3d110069347b3e27546a636e45e98885`: `SeasonalLakeEvolutionV2` integrates the existing `LakeReservoirV2.step` inflow/evaporation/overflow calculation with subsequent sediment deposition and displaced-water overflow. Immutable seasonal state carries changed bed elevations and retained volume through deterministic multi-season simulations; once a basin fills, later inflow and sediment are exported instead of silently stored.
- `4833daf599164468c5e6c2e5710a2be42768e070`: Seven lake infill JUnit regression methods.
- `d6e96a27f60050115826a31a0189776496e37042`: Five seasonal coupling JUnit regression methods.
- `3c8d894cddf64c5be2eb2e5572b70ebe68b0ecee`: Added one deterministic 500-case geometry sweep regression, for **13 new JUnit methods total**.

**Verification actually performed:** Java 21 `javac --release 17` compiled the exact new production sources and exact repository JUnit test sources against a locally recreated `LakeReservoirV2` implementation with matching public API and a minimal JUnit-compatible test harness. All **13 test methods passed locally**. Eight standalone smoke scenarios and **5,000 seeded lake geometry scenarios** also passed local water and sediment conservation checks. The four initially committed source/test Git blob SHAs were compared against the locally tested files and matched. **This is not an execution of Maven/JUnit or the full repository CI; the JUnit-compatible harness and recreated dependency do not prove repository-wide compatibility.** The 500-case regression was separately recompiled and passed after being committed.

**Explicit conservation examples:** A two-cell lake with 5 volume units of original capacity, 4 units of stored water, and 4,000 units of sediment mass at 2,000 mass/volume density ends with capacity 3, stored water 3, displaced overflow 1, and sediment retained 4,000. A season starting with 2 units, receiving 5, evaporating 1, and depositing the same sediment ends with stored 3 and overflow 3: `2 + 5 = 1 + 3 + 3`. Fully silted basins retain no lake water, and excess sediment is explicitly exported.

**Acceptance limitations / NOT FINISHED:** This is a **local, fixed-spill-elevation, caller-forced** lake evolution model, not automatic persistent planetary lake integration. It does not derive sediment loads from `SedimentBudgetV2` automatically, write updated terrain to durable world checkpoints, route displaced water or excess sediment into neighboring regions, recompute spillways/catchments after infill, model multiple outlets, validate sediment density against physical calibration, or derive evaporation and inflow from climate. Evaporation uses the existing explicit **start-of-step wetted-area** approximation, which may undercount evaporation when a dry lake fills during a season. No remote transfer authentication, durable global replay barrier/receipt compaction, erosion-induced in-flight route migration, groundwater, physically timed flooding, global drainage discovery, large-world endurance, or production save integration is completed. **Hydrology remains unfinished.**

**Next:** Independently inspect push-triggered CI for these commits and repair any failures; then connect the lake sediment and seasonal state to authoritative terrain/save checkpoints and conserved river flux. Preserve the outstanding cross-region replay and route-migration requirements before advancing other world layers. No merges, PRs, main-branch writes, production changes, or paid infrastructure changes.


**Additional numerical hardening (same run):** `899466ec2cf9fdac2359b73f1ced30ea2d231cef` rejects underflowing lake capacity × sediment density instead of incorrectly filling a lake with effectively zero sediment mass. `e4502b9dd404c5503cda6fd645ea25e75e3e25e7` adds the corresponding regression. The same 13 locally executed JUnit-compatible test methods passed after the correction. Full repository Maven/JUnit and push-triggered CI remain unverified.

## 2026-11-01 — CI repair and restartable lake-sediment snapshots

**Scope:** Only `feature/world-engine-v2`; no main-branch changes, merges, PRs, production deployments, or paid infrastructure.

**CI repair:** Inspected actual push-triggered GitHub Actions run [41258284115](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41258284115). Maven ran 756 tests, with one error in `HydrologyLakeSedimentSeasonsV2Test.refusesRepeatedOrUnknownSourcesAndBadRunoff`. The test tried to construct `Sample(1,2,100,100)`, which the global address constructor correctly rejects before the seasonal topology guard can execute. Commit `9a3555fda6bd2047c0c482590a12c117de821b06` uses the **valid but off-topology** sample `Sample(1,2,8,2)` instead. **CI passed** for this fix in [41262825492](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41262825492). The previously unverified lake sediment and multi-season tests are therefore included in a verified complete Maven run.

**New opt-in snapshot and restart functionality:**
- `HydrologyLakeSedimentSnapshotV2` encodes sediment-altered bed elevations and lake water storage in one deterministic, SHA-256-checksummed payload. A separately supplied immutable baseline binds lake cells, areas, spill heights, outlet identities, and river topology. Unknown lakes, changes to route edges, capacity-overflow storage, erosion of the original bed, altered cell areas and malformed snapshots fail closed. The checksum detects accidental corruption; **it does not authenticate the sender**.
- `HydrologyLakeSedimentSeasonsV2.resume` decodes a prior snapshot, advances a caller-supplied list of seasons and returns a new snapshot for caller-controlled atomic storage. It does not independently write a file or durably coordinate external water receipts. A zero-season resume leaves the canonical snapshot unchanged.
- `HydrologyLakeSedimentSnapshotV2Test` includes integration tests for sediment-driven capacity loss, restart and next-season conservation, split-run vs uninterrupted seasonal equivalence, enumeration-order stability, tampering/stale baseline, changed geometry/route rejection, invalid storage and a real local `HydrologyWorldRunnerV2` checkpoint round-trip. The runner test advances a zero-water marker, **not a full simultaneous rainfall+receipt+sediment transaction**.

**Commits this run:** `9a3555fd` (CI repair), `af5bcfa6` (snapshot codec), `75ce9d90` (compile correction), `a81bbff9` (snapshot tests), `491f6cf9` (canonical pole fixture), `e11d7404` (snapshot continuation), `efd5425f` (split-season regression), `5ffb79ed` (checkpoint restart regression), plus this documentation commit. The intermediate snapshot codec CI [41262882533](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41262882533) failed due to an invalid multi-catch; corrected `75ce9d90` passed [41262888707](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41262888707). The first snapshot test CI [41262906454](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41262906454) failed because its test fixture incorrectly treated all pole coordinates as canonical; corrected in `491f6cf9`. **The final new-test CI must be checked before claiming acceptance.** Push workflow history: https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions?query=branch%3Afeature%2Fworld-engine-v2.

**Still unfinished:** The snapshot codec is an explicit payload boundary, not automatic authoritative terrain geometry persistence or an atomic water/sediment/inbox epoch. It does not reconstruct new spillways or authenticate geographic topology. Fully silted basins still require a newly verified drainage graph. ## 2026-11-01 — Atomic sediment/lake/receipt epochs and checkpoint-local terrain binding

The runner restores the previous sediment geometry and lake water from its checkpoint, checks that each unique seasonal source preserves the exact zero-runoff baseline topology, stages available durable boundary receipts, injects them once before lake interception, evolves sediment/water, checks the coupled budgets, and atomically checkpoints the new lake geometry, retained water, epoch and receipt-delivery markers. It preserves future receipts. Full [Java CI 41266594481](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41266594481) **passed**, including **770 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**.
- `6cad0ccd18c184428e9f8aae8985093d3c021198` adds opt-in `HydrologyLakeSedimentTerrainBindingV2` and `advanceClosedSphericalLakeSedimentNetworkWithTerrain`. This mode requires the checkpoint's embedded world payload to be a canonical `HydrologyTerrainOverlayV2` and requires every existing lake-bed cell to match independently supplied seeded/overridden terrain **exactly**. It persists sediment-raised bed elevations and preserves unrelated terrain overrides in the **same checkpoint** as water storage and delivered receipts. Mismatched terrain/seed and invalid snapshot worlds abort without consuming receipts. Full [Java CI 41266667844](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41266667844) **passed**, including **773 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**.
- `2058683c4ba88ef5bb1ef8fb5d11c22fec76be32` adds a 100-season deterministic restart/receipt conservation regression, with nine deferred receipts delivered at their scheduled epochs, periodic checkpoint reopen, and comparison with an uninterrupted simulation. Its world experiment, finite-resource replay, browser-save checks, atlas export and real-browser verification also passed.

The tests cover exact-once receipt consumption, future-arrival parking, sediment displacement and downstream terminal outflow, checkpoint recovery, failed full-silting rollback, stale-writer protection, duplicate/changed source rejection, terrain/seed mismatch rollback, unchanged non-lake terrain overrides, two consecutive sediment-raising seasons, deterministic route enumeration, and 100-epoch restart equivalence. The complete Maven suite passed on GitHub Actions; **no local Maven execution is claimed**. All three code/test commits were verified by their own successful push-triggered workflows.

**Explicit remaining gaps and limits:** This is an **opt-in single-writer, closed-sphere, single-resolution** transaction on an independently supplied, verified, *unchanged* drainage plan. The terrain-binding mode makes lake beds consistent with the checkpoint-local terrain overlay, but does **not** write to a separate planet-wide authoritative terrain store or verify that caller-provided seed elevations match the real generated planet. Rainfall/receipt provenance must still be independently authenticated to prevent a source from being supplied twice. Exported river/lake sediment is not automatically transported into downstream beds; complete silting and erosion-induced spillway changes still require a new verified topology and reconciliation of water already in transit. Safe receipt-history compaction requires a durable replay watermark and is **not implemented**. Automatic planetary drainage discovery, distributed transfer authentication and receiver-loss recovery, multiple spillways, groundwater, calibrated seasonal climate, physically timed flooding, and large-world endurance tests are still unfinished. The 100-season test is a **small deterministic closed-sphere fixture**, not a planetary scale test. **Hydrology remains unfinished; continue these water tasks before other world layers.**


## 2026-11-01 — River sediment routing and lake interception (verification pending)

**Scope:** `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, `feature/world-engine-v2` exclusively. Starting HEAD `feade106c942b434ba01d195d78817fe15f73038`. Before editing, independently inspected [GitHub Actions run 41266771633](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41266771633): the `test` job completed with conclusion **success**, and its Maven test, world experiment, resource replay, browser save, atlas export and real-browser steps each reported **success**. **New opt-in implementation:**
- `1db71d219d39236f452eeb13a49828e5281d3530`: `HydrologyRiverSedimentTransportV2` routes caller-supplied eroded dry sediment mass along unique global river vertices in deterministic topological order. A per-vertex capacity limits onward transport; the remainder becomes explicitly accounted river-bed deposition. Every lake cell intercepts its upstream and local sediment once, and terminal exports and unloaded-frontier exports remain separate. Mass is accumulated with `BigDecimal`, with an exact internal conservation check. Duplicated sources, cycles, mixed resolution, invalid fluxes, unknown lake cells and overlapping lake ownership fail closed. The lake interception assumes **100% sediment trapping**; it is not a calibrated settling/transport model.

River-bed deposition and terminal sediment exports are **returned for later terrain/persistence integration**, not silently discarded or automatically written. Coupling refuses unresolved sediment frontiers and completely silted lakes requiring a new drainage topology.
- `714a94b1b25932dbaf97143c1b5a3fc6ff7b5c15`: Eight JUnit test methods cover upstream lake capture, separate downstream sediment export, capacity-limited river deposition, confluence deduplication, unloaded-neighbor pending sediment, deterministic enumeration, invalid/cyclic/overlapping graphs, and complete-silting fail-closed behavior.
- `bfbbad467795edeb58b4e7851f9ab8dc004b0d92`: A ninth JUnit method checks 200 seeded sediment mass/conservation and ordering scenarios.

## 2026-11-01 — Sediment frontier provenance and staged replay-resistant receiving (full CI pending)

**Scope and baseline:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, `feature/world-engine-v2`. Starting HEAD `b5c945de3354cffda0cb31f893ed15abb3fca7fd`. This is **baseline evidence**, not CI evidence for the new commits.

**Implemented (all opt-in, not yet durable):**
- `dda661a051ad834408bd2955db2d41f398997dd1`: `HydrologySedimentReceiptInboxV2` records a source epoch, scheduled arrival epoch, canonical geographic sender/receiver edge, mass, delivery markers and last claimed epoch. Exact replays are idempotent, conflicting quantities or different destinations from the same physical source/epoch fail closed, including aliases across sampling resolutions. Arrivals for unloaded destinations remain parked and future arrivals are not claimed early. Physical receiving vertices are uniquely owned across resolutions. Receipt aggregation uses `BigDecimal` and rejects overflow before marking delivery. Late *new* receipts for already closed arrival epochs are rejected, so senders must schedule future arrival epochs.
- `8d4f09a806ffa28e79a34cd7d5f4a950f4a3482f`: `HydrologySedimentReceiptRoutingV2` stages receipt claims with the existing imported river sediment and river/lake sediment solvers. - `165aca58c955aeb17a679699d9a3b5d08975f8f2`: `HydrologySedimentFrontierTransfersV2` derives uniquely identified sender-edge receipts from verified river transport. It checks their per-destination mass against the already calculated pending export and refuses unresolved positive-mass frontiers without a known receiving edge. The result is an **outbox proposal**, not an authenticated or persisted transfer.
- `bb0bfa51b362bf4e2c6387d4eb3a6b5b45da94ad`, `639e0e2814a4786771963a75871e26138fd33dd8`, `d07a7937f6db1171c0113adc128ffa4d883873f2`: 16 new JUnit test methods across three files. ## 2026-11-01 — Cross-region sediment conservation audit and atomic receipt batches (CI verified)

**Scope and starting state:** Work exclusively on `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, branch `feature/world-engine-v2`. Started from `214597ccb99ab949ec5bdfedb3b382cf72dd3ab6`. - `e4348508a1872ee41f63427f88d24fe925dc665c`: new opt-in `HydrologySedimentCrossRegionAuditV2` strictly reconciles a **complete supplied set** of sender frontier outbox proposals with receiving sediment receipt ledgers. Each dispatched physical source/epoch is counted once, including across different spherical sampling resolutions. Confluences preserve individual receipt provenance. Reports separately account for sediment still in transit, received but not yet claimed (including unloaded destinations), and durably marked delivered. A physical receiving vertex cannot be owned by two receiving regions, including cross-resolution aliases. Unknown or altered inbound receipts, repeated sender receipts, physical-source forks, invalid amounts and outbox-budget mismatches fail closed. `reconcileDurable` reads actual checksummed receiving checkpoint files before auditing; missing files fail rather than being assumed empty. The intervening `fb36f12cb65c8504809f07d295f776e172e9be0e` changed the seeded test to use the exact decimal sum required by the outbox contract.
- `d64aca7b00c092ec6c5b4382712a6c2da9d4c163`: `HydrologySedimentCheckpointV2.receiveBatch` atomically stages an entire map of future sediment receipts in deterministic order before performing **one** guarded local checkpoint write. A late new arrival, physical-source fork, altered replay or stale writer cannot commit an earlier subset. Exact historical replays remain idempotent. This is single-writer local-file checkpoint atomicity, not a distributed sender/receiver transaction.
- `6788f06acbd9c50a60c3f2af3070d7913d98b963`: six JUnit methods verify batch persistence and restart, rejection of late and forked receipts without partial writes, byte-for-byte deterministic checkpoint contents regardless of map insertion order, stale-writer rollback, and replay after delivery.

Persist the sender outbox atomically with exported river sediment, prove receiving checkpoint delivery, and durably record sender acknowledgments. Recover safely when either checkpoint is lost. No cross-region authentication exists.
2. **Joint water/sediment/terrain transaction:** the guarded lake-only sediment checkpoint still rejects pending water receipts, riverbed erosion/deposition, terminal export and sediment requiring a new drainage route. Riverbed previews must be checked against **all** spherical neighboring routes before authoritative terrain commits. It is not an online distributed consistency protocol and does not infer unknown transfers or authenticate senders. Unloaded destinations are represented as awaiting delivery only after their receipt has reached a receiving ledger; before that they are in transit.

## 2026-11-01 — Durable sediment delivery coordination and crash-gap recovery (CI verified)

**Scope:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, branch `feature/world-engine-v2`. Starting HEAD `6e366028e936ecc0b06b8e4a77afe0d8e8316a78`. At the start of this run, the latest push-triggered [Java CI 41295045544](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41295045544) was independently inspected and **passed with 856 Maven/JUnit tests, 0 failures/errors/skips**. This supersedes the prior section's unverified status for receiver recovery: those nine methods were included in that green baseline.

### Implementation and commits

- `56650b82c6bb31c4f8503c1d4c6143be85f6ba85`: added opt-in `HydrologySedimentTransferCoordinatorV2`. `deliverAndAcknowledge` reads an already durable sender outbox, selects only receipts owned by the specified physical receiver vertices, checks conflicts and closed epochs before writes, persists a deterministic receiver batch, then records receiver-backed sender acknowledgments. Exact retries do not double-count mass or re-acknowledge receipts. Foreign/unloaded destinations remain in the sender outbox. It detects sender/receiver file aliases.
- The same class adds `recoverAndAcknowledge`: checks distinct sender journals, uses `HydrologySedimentReceiverRecoveryV2.repairBatch` to reconcile the **complete caller-supplied** sender history in one receiver checkpoint, then acknowledges each sender in deterministic key order. Acknowledgments are independent local writes, so a crash between them requires a retry; the receiver checkpoint is never rolled back by the coordinator.
- `a026793705989cbc26a63067fe15b1ea51f85824`: added ten JUnit tests for durable delivery, idempotence, unloaded destinations, two-sender confluences, receiver failure rollback, closed epochs, conflicting mass, receiver backup restoration after acknowledgment, physical ownership/file aliases, duplicate sender files and 70 seeded transfers.
- `1dc907f8a92a051a50586492e8f956f5374e110c`: corrected the cross-resolution physical-vertex test fixture. The earlier [CI 41299432664](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41299432664) failed **one** test because the original fixture represented a different geographic vertex, not because of a source compilation failure. The corrected [CI 41299442641](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41299442641) **passed all 866 tests**.
- `51375446692e7d107b3e874f868988bc1425ff23`: added two crash-gap regression tests: receiver checkpoint committed before sender acknowledgment, and partial acknowledgment across multiple senders. Both prove retry convergence without re-receiving sediment.

### Verified acceptance evidence

- [Final implementation CI 41299545494](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41299545494) **passed**: **868 Maven/JUnit tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**. This is **12 new JUnit methods** relative to the 856-test starting baseline. The dedicated `HydrologySedimentTransferCoordinatorV2Test` suite passed all 12 methods. Java build, world experiment, resource replay, browser-save checks, atlas export and real-browser atlas verification completed successfully.
- Deterministic receiver ownership uses geographic normalization rather than sample-object equality. Incoming sediment is counted once per durable receipt, and mass remains in sender history after acknowledgment for replay recovery. A transfer is acknowledged only after the receiver checkpoint persists the exact mass.
- Local-file atomicity is **per checkpoint only**. The two files are not committed in one distributed transaction. A crash between receiver and sender writes is recoverable by idempotent replay **provided the receiver epoch remains open for any missing receipt**. The tests exercise the ordinary crash gap, partial sender acknowledgments, and recovery from a restored older receiver checkpoint.

### Remaining hydrology acceptance gaps

1. **Authoritative integration:** the new coordinator is opt-in; the world runner does not automatically dispatch sediment, authenticate senders, discover authoritative receiver ownership, or schedule cross-region delivery/recovery. Sender outbox persistence must be coordinated with the terrain epoch that generated exported sediment.
2. **Closed-epoch disaster recovery:** if a receiver loses a previously committed receipt and has already advanced past its arrival epoch, replay correctly fails closed. A full checkpoint/epoch rollback and re-simulation protocol, plus trusted durable backups, is not implemented.
3. **Cross-region security and concurrency:** no authenticated network transport, interprocess locking, global transaction, or cross-region exactly-once guarantee. Caller-supplied complete sender sets and physical ownership are trusted preconditions.
4. **Replay-safe compaction:** sender/receiver histories remain unbounded until independently durable global replay barriers and watermarks can prove safe truncation.
5. **Authoritative erosion/topology:** riverbed sediment erosion/deposition is still a guarded preview, not a joint terrain/lake/water/sediment/receipt checkpoint. In-flight water/sediment migration across changed drainage routes and multiple spillways remains unresolved.
6. **Planetary physics:** automatic full-world drainage discovery, groundwater, calibrated seasonal climate and sediment transport, physically timed flooding, and large-world endurance/physical validation remain unfinished.

**Safety:** No merges, PRs, writes to `main` or other branches, production changes or paid infrastructure changes. **Hydrology is NOT complete.** Continue hydrology before other world layers.

A crash, I/O failure or concurrent writer after preflight can leave a subset of loaded receivers committed; durable receipt IDs support retry convergence. ## 2026-11-01 — Read-only topology-change gate for durable water and sediment (CI verified)

**Scope:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck` branch `feature/world-engine-v2`. Starting HEAD `7081ce0e1d4190168e7250ec90f42a57a1e0fddc`. The previous [Java CI 41308380069](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41308380069) passed 882 Maven/JUnit tests before these changes.

Its read-only `inspect` first invokes the existing complete-network durable preflight, then reads checksummed sender journals and receiver checkpoints to classify sediment as **in transit**, **received but awaiting consumption**, or **consumed**. It also identifies missing sender acknowledgments and pending water receipts in the supplied receiving checkpoints. It counts sediment mass once at the sender and normalizes receiving vertices geographically.
- The opt-in `requireQuiescent` rejects a proposed drainage-topology change while any inspected sediment is in transit or awaiting consumption, any sender acknowledgment remains outstanding, or any supplied receiver has pending water. It fails closed when a previously acknowledged sediment receipt is missing from a restored receiver checkpoint. This is a **precondition check**, not a terrain mutation or distributed lock.
- `1acb6273387f34f86958d3e83449c3e6ba8047c7`: added **six JUnit regression methods** covering in-transit to awaiting to consumed progression, unloaded destination retention, receiver restore after acknowledgment, consumed-but-unacknowledged sediment, pending water, and cross-resolution shared-vertex ownership rejection. No interprocess lock, authentication, global transaction or world-runner integration has been added. The gate does **not** commit any erosion, deposition, lake or drainage-topology change.
3. A consumed and acknowledged receipt is still retained for replay recovery. No historical receipt compaction, globally durable replay watermark, trusted backup recovery floor, or closed-epoch disaster rollback is implemented.
4. Riverbed terrain mutation and in-flight route migration remain blocked until joint water/sediment/lake/terrain checkpoint semantics and independent route validation are implemented.

It is not wired into the authoritative world runner's region load/unload lifecycle, does not automatically discover the complete sender set or all physical ownership, and does not orchestrate all loaded lake regions as one atomic world step.
2. **Not a distributed transaction:** sender receipt delivery/acknowledgment and the subsequent lake checkpoint are separate durable writes. Preflight can race with another writer; there is no authenticated network transport, interprocess lock or global exactly-once guarantee. Failed lake epochs after delivery retain sediment receipts for retry; missing closed-epoch history still requires trusted rollback/resimulation.
3. **Riverbed erosion/deposition still preview-only:** this milestone atomically binds **lake beds**, not riverbed incision/deposition. Joint authoritative riverbed/terrain/water/sediment/export checkpoints and verified alternative spherical drainage routes are not implemented.
4. **No replay-history compaction:** sender and receiver histories remain unbounded without independent durable replay barriers, global watermarks and trusted backup floors.
5. **Incomplete planetary hydrology:** topology migration of in-flight water/sediment, automatic global drainage discovery, multiple spillways, groundwater, calibrated seasonal forcing/sediment physics, physically timed flooding, and large-world endurance validation remain unfinished.

**Safety:** No merge, PR, write to `main` or another branch, production change or paid infrastructure operation. **Hydrology is NOT complete. Continue these water tasks before other world layers.**

Acknowledged receipts stay in durable history for explicit disaster recovery.
6. `9c7b8a3a` added **six JUnit tests** for deterministic load order, shared destination deduplication, cross-resolution already-loaded suppression, mixed-resolution refusal before invoking a provider, duplicate sender rejection, and load-budget fail-fast.

These 910 tests cover all source/test changes in this milestone. The documentation-only commit itself may trigger a separate CI run.

### Unfinished acceptance criteria and safety limits

1. **No automatic authoritative runner integration:** the multi-lake scheduler and sediment-driven load hints are opt-in. The runtime must still discover complete sender/receiver ownership, integrate actual region loading/unloading, and schedule water and sediment jointly. A hint is not an authenticated transfer or a verified downstream route.
2. **No distributed atomicity or concurrency barrier:** each receiver's receipt delivery, sender acknowledgment and lake/terrain epoch are separate local checkpoint writes. A crash can leave a partial multi-region epoch; same-target retries are supported only while durable sender histories and open/recoverable epochs remain intact. Concurrent writers require external serialization.
3. **No mixed-resolution unloaded region directory:** load hints fail closed for pending destinations at incompatible resolutions; they do not invent a region ownership mapping. Acknowledged transfers lost from a restored receiver still need explicit recovery.
4. **Riverbed erosion/deposition still not atomically committed:** existing sediment routing previews river deposits and terminal export, but authoritative riverbed terrain, water, sediment and export must be bound and audited together before enabling terrain changes.
5. **Replay-history compaction and topology migration remain unfinished:** no globally durable replay watermark/backup floor, no safe transfer rerouting across changed drainage, and no closed-epoch rollback/resimulation protocol.
6. **Broader hydrology remains incomplete:** automatic planetary catchment discovery, multiple lake spillways, groundwater, calibrated seasonal forcing, physically timed flooding, authenticated interprocess transport, and large-world endurance/physics tests are not complete.

**Safety:** No merges, PRs, writes to `main` or other branches, production changes, or paid infrastructure operations. Hydrology remains the priority before other world layers.

## 2026-11-02 — Deterministic multi-region riverbed epochs and shared-halo preflight (source CI verified)

**Scope:** `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, branch `feature/world-engine-v2` only. Starting HEAD `88a4cde5e841fc9690e7ed0f68f7c7eb4f4b4f6b`. [Starting CI 41348639699](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41348639699) completed successfully with **959 Maven/JUnit tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, `BUILD SUCCESS`, and successful browser/atlas checks. This also independently verifies the previous run's ten riverbed-runner tests, which had been marked pending.

### Implemented

1. `HydrologyRiverbedLoadedNetworkEpochV2` (`7a1c7ca3789d91e18c93975528f83e5db3eafcf3`) coordinates a caller-supplied complete, quiescent network of independently durable **closed-budget** riverbed epochs. It checks sender/receiver checkpoint histories, physical ownership, all region budgets and strictly route-verified local erosion/deposition **before the first write**, then commits in deterministic sorted region order. This is opt-in, not an automatic world-loop scheduler.
2. Recovery requires each region's **original checksummed checkpoint** and identical inputs. A region already at the exact expected committed checkpoint is skipped, while a changed world, different terrain result, stale checkpoint, altered sediment ledger or unexpected epoch is rejected. This permits safe retries after one region commits and another fails, without treating an epoch number alone as proof of idempotence. Each region remains a separate atomic local-file write.
3. Cross-region edits must be owned by exactly one physical receiver, including shared geographic vertices represented at different resolutions. The batch sums local erosion/deposition budgets with decimal arithmetic and rejects unbalanced, open, exported, imported or lake-captured sediment budgets through the existing mass-balance/receipt gates.
4. Shared spherical one-ring halo validation (`8bf34ad1249222ad85749b545d4ad5f0bc1e57a1`) compares each overlapping physical sample's **before and after** elevation across all region plans. It rejects inconsistent seeded elevations, mismatched cross-LOD aliases and neighbor edits invisible to another region's routing guard. This is a conservative safety restriction, **not** global drainage re-solving.

### Tests and verified CI

- `HydrologyRiverbedLoadedNetworkEpochV2Test` (`1fae1ad00d714e48f9fe80076dec47c8c10d034c`) adds **seven** integration tests for sorted preflight, exact conservation and durable terrain changes, complete replay, interrupted-batch recovery, invalid later-region rollback, duplicate/unowned physical vertices, altered replay parameters and parked water.
- `641536a64db9b36872b731a8de59ba73e80d69fc` adds **one** test for inconsistent adjacent spherical halos; `bc64a5321ae968d8e8e56c88936e305561df30fd` adds **one** test rejecting unproven parked sediment.
- [Initial batch test CI 41354166474](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41354166474): **966 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- [Shared-halo integration CI 41354233521](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41354233521): **967 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- [Final source/test CI 41354289769](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41354289769): **968 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`. The real-browser atlas verification also passed. This source run precedes the present documentation commit; the documentation commit's CI must be checked separately.
- Five implementation/test commits between the starting HEAD and the final source commit changed only the new hydrology coordinator and its test file. No CI failures required correction in this run.

### Explicit remaining hydrology acceptance gaps

1. **Not a globally atomic batch:** a crash after the first local checkpoint can leave some regions at the new epoch and others at the old one. Exact-state replay repairs this only when all original checkpoint baselines and complete sender histories are retained, and no concurrent writer has intervened. The caller must hold an external single-writer barrier. No authenticated transport or durable global replay/compaction watermark exists.
2. **No cross-region river sediment imports/exports in the riverbed epoch:** every individual riverbed budget must be local and closed. Pending/unloaded destinations, lake capture, multi-region mass transfers, changed catchment topology and displaced in-transit water/sediment still need a coupled transaction and verified routing migration.
3. **No automatic combined world epoch:** region loading, durable water discharge delivery, sediment sender acknowledgment, lake advancement and riverbed commits are still caller-driven rather than one authoritative scheduled lifecycle.
4. **Global physics still incomplete:** globally authoritative ocean outlets/catchment discovery, mixed-resolution region directory, multiple lake spillways, groundwater, calibrated seasonal water balance, physically timed flooding, erosion/sediment capacity calibration and planetary-scale endurance testing are unfinished.
5. Shared-halo checks are a conservative **one-ring consistency gate**, not proof of globally stable drainage, lake spillways or watershed identities. All region plans must use consistent authoritative elevation data. Tests do not establish planetary-scale performance or distributed fault tolerance.

**Safety:** No merge, PR, write to `main` or any other branch, production operation or paid infrastructure change. **Hydrology remains unfinished and is the priority for subsequent runs.**


## 2026-11-02 — Exact lake replay recovery and cross-region water receipt audit (source CI verified)

**Scope:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck` branch `feature/world-engine-v2`. Starting HEAD `899b18daf978c852745b6577a1ec8db7ddbdbbd0`; [starting Java CI 41354399611](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41354399611) passed **968 Maven/JUnit tests**, zero failures/errors/skips. No other branches, PRs, production or paid infrastructure were touched.

### Implemented and exercised

1. **Exact, read-only lake checkpoint simulation:** `HydrologySedimentCheckpointV2.planLakeWithTerrain` factors the previously write-only lake/terrain/sediment calculation into a pure checkpoint plan. The existing atomic lake commit uses this same plan and its guarded single-file write. It retains previous lake volume, sediment receipts, water inbox and terrain validation. Initial refactor commit `405e586756d5edd2ccab017679440f189928a209`; the missing checked `IOException` propagation was corrected in `ecaf4f2ffdddd644a9267199982df305118fbc07`.
2. **Strict interrupted-lake replay:** New `HydrologySedimentLakeReplayVerifierV2` (`e0763936679cf12f261c307feb009c15826895cb`) rebuilds the exact expected next checkpoint from an ORIGINAL checksummed region checkpoint, complete durable sender histories and the supplied lake/terrain/seasonal forcing. It accepts an unchanged pre-epoch receiver, or a receiver with only a provable subset of staged sender receipts after an interrupted delivery, or a completed checkpoint **only when the entire persisted next checkpoint equals the recomputed result**. Missing receipts, altered water/lake/terrain results, unexpected inbox state or changed original baseline reject before continuing. Different parameters producing exactly the same checkpoint cannot be distinguished: this is **state replay verification, not cryptographic input attestation**.
3. **Opt-in verified multi-region lake lifecycle:** `HydrologySedimentLoadedLakeNetworkEpochV2.planVerified` and `advanceVerified` (`34e49e9ead75571585a355112018092b643258a4`) require one retained original checkpoint per loaded region. All regions undergo exact-state preflight before the first write, and committed regions are verified again after the batch. The existing non-strict `plan/advance` API remains available for compatibility. Delivery/acknowledgment and lake commits remain **separate local-file transactions**, and the caller must serialize writers and retain complete histories.
4. **Water conservation and ownership audit:** New `HydrologyLoadedWaterAuditV2` (`d34ff9f4c11127178b4d15a63702202e8bd4b313`) reads durable water inboxes from loaded receivers, enforces unique canonical physical destination ownership (including cross-resolution aliases), rejects duplicate transfer identities and physical source forks in the same epoch, and sums accepted/delivered/pending volumes with `BigDecimal` without counting shared vertices twice. It checks delivery markers and consumed epochs against committed epochs. `requireSettled` rejects parked receipts. This is a **receiver-side ledger audit**, not proof of the completeness or authenticity of external sender journals or a full hydrologic mass balance.
5. **Wired preflight gates:** `HydrologyRiverbedLoadedNetworkEpochV2.plan` now requires the water audit after sediment quiescence (`96bf66acd23dad6a7f274203f1667a10706a09bf`); strict lake preflight also requires settled, nonforked water receipts (`d0dbe771468e31a1a22d11784627aad4ef24d36a`). These changes do not automatically deliver water or advance seasons.
6. **Tests:** `HydrologySedimentLakeReplayVerifierV2Test` (`07dc9099bbe6a2f521652605ee55d5c3ac2d55d7`) adds six tests for exact read-only planning, restart/replay, changed forcing, receipt-only crash recovery, invalid baselines and unproven receipts. `HydrologyLoadedWaterAuditV2Test` (`5e00c59c389e269246c86bd6d36b7d06bb1d9306`) adds six tests for mass accounting, settlement, source forks, cross-resolution physical aliases, unowned arrivals/file aliases and impossible consumed epochs. Two more tests (`f3e32a0dcfea41250a8928a6026e37a4510f03d4`) exercise exact two-region lake recovery and interrupted first-region commit. Source documentation clarification commits: `1eb1677b8f2a016a9cbbfb5033da3e20e436c203`, `439727e77059caa68c485d49ef1a62a41e592327`.

### Exact verification

- Initial CI runs [41359051145](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359051145), [41359073028](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359073028), [41359087201](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359087201), and [41359125326](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359125326) failed to compile because the newly extracted read-only lake planner did not declare an `IOException` from terrain binding. This was **fixed**, not ignored.
- [Corrected lake planner CI 41359154501](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359154501): **974 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- [Water audit test CI 41359227453](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359227453): **980 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- [Integrated preflight CI 41359244315](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359244315): **980 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`.
- [Final 14-new-test source CI 41359275439](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359275439): **982 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, 0 failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`; planet atlas export and real-browser atlas verification passed. This run verifies source and tests through `f3e32a0dcfea41250a8928a6026e37a4510f03d4`. The subsequent comment-only commits and this documentation commit have separate CI runs; check them independently rather than assuming success.

### Explicit remaining hydrology acceptance gaps

1. **Automatic combined epoch scheduling remains unfinished.** Loaded-region discovery, water receipt delivery, sediment sender acknowledgments, lake evolution and riverbed erosion are still caller-driven. The new strict lake recovery is opt-in and requires original checkpoints plus complete sender histories.
2. **No global atomicity or authenticated sender provenance.** A crash may leave some regions advanced and others pending. Exact-state recovery works only with retained originals, complete journals and an external single-writer barrier. No globally durable replay watermark or safe sender/receiver receipt-history compaction exists.
3. **Cross-region sediment/riverbed coupling remains closed-budget only.** Riverbed epochs cannot consume remote sediment imports, export to unloaded destinations, capture material in lakes, or migrate in-transit water/sediment when terrain changes routes. The strict one-ring halo guard does not reconstruct global catchments.
4. **Planetary physical hydrology remains incomplete.** Authoritative global ocean/coastline and catchment discovery, mixed-LOD ownership directory, multiple spillways, groundwater, calibrated seasonal climate and lake forcing, timed floods, sediment transport capacity calibration and planetary-scale endurance testing remain outstanding.
5. **Water audit limitations:** It verifies persisted receiver-side identities and totals, but does not establish whether a sender failed to dispatch water, authenticate remote senders, or certify global conservation without complete upstream inventories. It cannot authorize history deletion.

**Safety:** Only `feature/world-engine-v2` was modified. No merge, PR, write to `main` or another branch, production operation or paid infrastructure change. **Hydrology remains unfinished; continue hydrology before other world layers.**


## 2026-11-02 — Multi-sender mixed-resolution water delivery, loaded-source ownership and consumed-water audit (CI verified)

**Scope:** `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck`, branch `feature/world-engine-v2` only. Starting HEAD `0f99d1aec8ba2010c2ee382f15831a11c54cf9e8`. [Starting Java CI 41359414102](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41359414102) completed successfully with **982 Maven/JUnit tests**, zero failures/errors/skips. Only hydrology implementation, test and this progress document were modified.

### Verified additions

1. **Durable, multi-sender water preflight and dispatch:** New `HydrologyMixedResolutionLoadedNetworkV2` (`7a87523fa3c825f8d1962bc7a9bc054bcdcd5961`) audits a caller-declared set of mixed-LOD sender checkpoints and loaded receiving regions before any writes. It rejects duplicate sender identities, forked physical source routes, cross-LOD duplicate physical receiver ownership, aliased files, unowned/unproven receiver receipts, mismatched volumes and acknowledged receipts absent from loaded receivers. The network dispatches selected transfers in deterministic physical-edge order through the existing durable receiver-first, sender-acknowledgment-second transfer mechanism. Unloaded destinations remain in sender outboxes. Cross-region delivery remains independently durable, not one distributed transaction.
2. **Water mass accounting and replay safety:** The read-only report tracks exported, loaded-destination, unloaded-destination, accepted and acknowledged volumes using `BigDecimal`, with a distinct pending-loaded transfer count. Recovery after receiver acceptance but before sender acknowledgment is idempotent. No receipt history is pruned. `e76f9e4ac1f1a1e256f40bd67937ac4a9a2f6e76` preserves already acknowledged histories when their receiving region is currently unloaded; such receipts are reverified when the receiver reloads, not declared globally settled while absent.
3. **Loaded senders and consumed water:** `6cfc7240623712b6a754bc2f5688dba9f55b6411` allows a region to appear as both a sender and receiver under the same authoritative key and checkpoint, while rejecting cross-name file aliases. A physically loaded source must belong to its actual sender checkpoint, and a locally owned edge cannot be reimported as remote discharge. The report now separately accounts for **delivered versus parked accepted water**. This is receiver-side receipt accounting, not a full water-cycle mass balance or global sender discovery.
4. **Tests:** `3ee52689637befbbd0fc4f3a366adc43c1cbc57e` and `7b71010abd18aece321a66dcef991c1e067a7200` added **16 JUnit integration tests** covering two-sender confluence, mixed-LOD physical aliases, unloaded-region deferral and subsequent delivery, replay after interrupted acknowledgment, whole-batch preflight rollback, duplicate ownership, source forks, missing acknowledgments, missing sender histories, deterministic ordering, loaded sender ownership, already-acknowledged unloaded histories, and delivered/parked water. `26b48ed668d5623d42dc41e64b384076b56fcbb9` corrected a Java test declaration that used `var` in a compound declaration.

### Remaining acceptance gaps (hydrology still unfinished)

1. **Not automatically scheduled:** This mixed-resolution water network API is opt-in and not yet wired into one authoritative world-loop lifecycle with region loading, sediment delivery/acknowledgment, lake advancement and riverbed erosion. Existing regular water-inbox and mixed-resolution water-outbox formats are separate; this coordinator does not silently bridge them.

A crash can leave a partial batch, recoverable only with retained sender histories and cooperative single-writer coordination. A caller may omit a sender or an unloaded receiver, and no read-only local audit can prove global completeness. There is no durable global replay barrier, safe receipt-history compaction, authenticated transfer channel or cross-machine fencing.

## 2026-11-02 — Coupled loaded water/sediment transfers, cross-LOD unloaded hints, topology gate (CI verified)

**Scope and baseline:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck` on `feature/world-engine-v2`. Started at `2ad59c445dccd542810fae23c8e079da8c2cdf60`; [baseline CI 41364596953](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41364596953) passed **998 Maven/JUnit tests**, 0 failures/errors/skips and all world, browser-save, atlas and browser checks.

### Implemented and verified

1. **Coupled durable loaded-region transfer preflight and delivery:** New `HydrologyCoupledLoadedTransfersV2` validates a common authoritative physical ownership map, water and sediment checkpoint inventories, cross-media file aliases, cross-resolution shared vertices, and both existing network preflights **before any receipt write**. It dispatches water receipts first, then sediment receipts, with each medium retaining its existing receiver-first acknowledgment and crash retry semantics. The result exposes separate water-volume and sediment-mass budgets. This is opt-in and not a distributed transaction or automatic world-loop scheduler.
2. **Deterministic unloaded frontier hints:** `pendingUnloaded` groups unacknowledged water and sediment receipts by physical global coordinate, even across LOD aliases, without double-counting a vertex. It reports water volume and sediment mass separately, excludes loaded destinations and acknowledged histories, and returns deterministic coordinate order. It deliberately does **not** guess an owning tile/resolution, load a region, prune history, or discover unknown downstream terrain.
3. **Fail-closed terrain/topology quiescence gate:** `requireTopologyQuiescence` rejects drainage edits while any supplied mixed-resolution water is unloaded, unacknowledged or unconsumed. It also invokes the existing sediment topology gate to require acknowledged and consumed sediment plus no pending water in the sediment checkpoints. This is a **read-only check**, not permission for a concurrent or globally atomic terrain edit; the caller must provide all sender histories and freeze writers.
4. **Regression coverage:** Added **16 JUnit tests** for two-media conservation and idempotent retries, recovery after water-only partial delivery, invalid second-medium preflight rollback, unloaded deferral, cross-LOD physical aliases, deterministic hints, acknowledged-history exclusion, ownership/file alias rejection, and topology gating before and after water consumption.

### Exact verification and correction

- [Source/test CI 41369429016](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41369429016) at `fef060ce13c85fa10bb3745590cab77b37854373`: **1014 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, **0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**, `BUILD SUCCESS`. All **16** `HydrologyCoupledLoadedTransfersV2Test` tests passed. The smallest world experiment, resource replay, browser-save controls, planet atlas export and real-browser atlas verification also passed.
- [Intermediate CI 41369329488](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41369329488) and [41369389786](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41369389786) failed because test fixtures reused a sender checkpoint path as a receiver path. Corrected the fixture names in `fef060ce`, and the final source/test CI passed. [Implementation CI 41369219853](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41369219853) also passed before the new tests.
- Comparison from baseline to `fef060ce`: **7 commits**, changing exactly **two hydrology files** (new coordinator and test). Documentation is a separate eighth commit and its own workflow must be checked before calling that workflow green.

### Remaining acceptance gaps (hydrology NOT complete)

1. The coupled coordinator is opt-in and only handles delivery/acknowledgment for **two distinct durable checkpoint formats**. The regular water-inbox format and mixed-resolution water outbox are not automatically bridged. No authoritative lifecycle yet coordinates region loading, mixed-resolution water, sediment, lake storage, seasonal forcing, riverbed erosion, and topology migration in one scheduled world epoch.
2. Multi-file transfers remain individually atomic, **not globally atomic**. A crash after water delivery but before sediment delivery leaves a partial batch recoverable only with complete retained sender histories and cooperative single-writer discipline. There is no authenticated cross-machine transport, durable global fencing or replay-history compaction.
3. Unloaded hints require an external authoritative mixed-LOD region directory and actual terrain/route provider. They do not infer ocean outlets, global catchment closure or stable basin identities beyond already verified terminal anchors.
4. Riverbed epochs still require closed local sediment budgets; coupled cross-region imported/exported sediment in terrain commits and migration of in-transit material when drainage changes are unfinished. The quiescence gate does not execute or globally serialize topology changes.
5. Planetary ocean/coastline routing, multiple lake spillways, groundwater, calibrated multi-year seasonal forcing, physically timed flooding, capacity-calibrated erosion, planetary endurance and distributed fault-injection testing remain incomplete.

**Safety:** No PR, merge, change to `main` or other branches, production operation or paid infrastructure change. Continue hydrology on `feature/world-engine-v2` before other world layers.

### Changes

1. **Opt-in durable sender-history fence:** New `HydrologyCoupledSenderFenceV2` captures the exact sender file inventory, water outbox and inbox/world/epoch, and sediment sender journal before an external directory is consulted. It allows only monotonic growth of receipt acknowledgments during delivery. `HydrologyCoupledFrontierResolutionV2.deliverBatch`, `deliverAll`, and `deliverBatches` now reject producer mutation during preflight; the multi-batch drain also verifies the original sender history before and after each batch. This prevents a changing sender frontier from silently bypassing the frozen directory. It does **not** lock independent processes or make partially committed batches globally atomic. Commits: `c9e67797`, `9a751505`, test `8fdf7065`.
2. **Seasonal rain/snow/soil balance in physical units:** New `HydrologySeasonalSnowSoilV2` partitions precipitation at the freezing point, accumulates snow, bounds positive-temperature degree-day melt by existing snow, bounds evapotranspiration by available liquid water, retains water up to an explicit soil capacity, and exports excess as runoff. A decimal-based audit checks snow + soil + precipitation = new snow + new soil + evaporation + runoff. Explicit SI units and finite/overflow validation avoid silently mixing depths with volumes. This is an **opt-in local catchment calculation**, not calibrated climate, global routing, groundwater, or a persistent seasonal world scheduler. Commits: `9f3d27fb`, tests `9d16a34a`, overflow fix `b69a01cd`.
3. **Ten new JUnit tests committed:** Four sender-history mutation/acknowledgment integration tests and six seasonal water-budget tests, including freezing precipitation, bounded snowmelt, soil capacity, evaporation, deterministic multi-season replay, and invalid/overflow forcing.

### Exact verification and limits

- **Verified baseline:** [CI 41379752928](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41379752928), 1,032 Maven/JUnit tests passed on the previous HEAD.
- **Isolated verification this run:** Java 17 compilation of the sender-fence API against shape-compatible stubs passed. An equivalent Java 17 seasonal implementation compiled and passed five standalone smoke assertions for freezing, melt, evaporation, deterministic season sequencing and overflow. **These are not full repository tests.**
- **Not yet verified:** The ten new JUnit tests, complete Maven build, and GitHub Actions result for the new source/test HEAD. The connected commit-run lookup exposes PR-triggered runs only and returned no push-run results; no local Maven checkout was available. Do not claim a new green CI run until the exact new HEAD's workflow and logs are checked. Fix any CI failures before treating these changes as accepted.

### Still unfinished (water subsystem NOT complete)

1. Automatic authoritative lifecycle for region loading, coupled water/sediment delivery, lake storage, climate forcing and riverbed epochs. The sender fence is cooperative read-only verification, **not** a distributed lock or global transaction; a writer can still race between verification and a checkpoint write. Incomplete multi-region commits require retained sender histories and replay.
2. Coupled cross-region sediment imports/exports in riverbed terrain commits; safe rerouting/migration of in-transit water and sediment after terrain/topology changes; replay-safe compaction and durable global fencing.
3. Authoritative planetary drainage, stable resolved catchments and verified ocean/coastline mouths beyond existing known terminals; multiple lake spillways and groundwater.
4. Calibrated precipitation/temperature/evaporation/melt parameters, physically timed floods, lake/soil/snow integration, erosion-capacity calibration, large-world endurance and distributed fault injection.

**Safety:** Only development-branch commits; no PR, merge, main/other branch write, production operation or paid infrastructure change. Continue hydrology before other world layers.


## 2026-11-02 — Sediment sender epoch fencing and independently audited seasonal runoff

**Scope:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck` branch `feature/world-engine-v2`. Starting HEAD `2bedd9d1749c9e420b1d70819c1f7cf44e99888f`. The previously unverified snow/soil and sender-fence commits are now **verified** by [Java CI 41385380189](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41385380189): **1,042 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`, and atlas browser checks passed.

### Implemented and verified

1. **Stronger sender checkpoint fencing:** `HydrologyCoupledSenderFenceV2` now captures sediment sender checkpoint epochs in addition to immutable receipt histories and acknowledged receipt sets. During a coupled drain, sediment epoch movement must be explained by growth in acknowledged receipts, bounded by the number of newly acknowledged receipts. A silent epoch advance with an unchanged sediment journal is rejected. Duplicate normalized sender file paths (including two names for the same water or sediment checkpoint) are rejected during capture, preventing a single physical sender from being treated as multiple independent senders. Commits `768b47ef` and `e8bf728f`.
2. **Independent SI-volume seasonal checkpoint audit:** New `HydrologySeasonalBudgetAuditV2` compares the canonical depth-based `HydrologySnowSoilStateV2` transition against the separate `HydrologySeasonalSnowSoilV2` volume bucket for each globally canonical cell. It verifies snow/soil storage, per-cell river runoff sources, source uniqueness, precipitation, evaporation, total runoff and overall conservation. It rejects mismatched areas, forged stored water, altered runoff and accidental precipitation reinjection. `HydrologySnowSoilStateV2.advanceAudited` exposes an **opt-in** audited transition; the original `advance` behavior remains unchanged. Commits `8df82b81`, `1be699b5`, `3ff0e196`, `116ccfb6`.
3. **Eight new JUnit regression tests:** Three sender-fence tests cover silent sediment epoch drift during directory preflight, direct fence verification and duplicate checkpoint aliases. Five seasonal audit tests cover two seasons, tampered runoff, tampered storage/double-counting, area drift/duplicate source, and deterministic audited advancement.

### Exact CI evidence

- [Sender implementation CI 41390830093](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41390830093): passed; 1,042 Maven/JUnit tests.
- [Sender regression CI 41390840495](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41390840495): passed.
- [Independent seasonal audit CI 41390887336](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41390887336): passed; 1,049 Maven/JUnit tests, zero failures/errors/skips.
- [Final source/test CI 41390921211](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41390921211) at `116ccfb6236bae9075a189cea71332505f75fc9e`: **passed**, **1,050 Maven/JUnit tests**, zero failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`; world experiment, resource replay, browser save, atlas export and real-browser atlas checks passed. This is the acceptance run for all source and test changes above.
- This documentation-only commit triggers its own workflow; verify that separately. Do not treat a previous commit's green CI as proof of a later source change.

### Outstanding acceptance gaps: hydrology NOT finished

1. **Authoritative lifecycle:** Automatic region loading, coupled water/sediment delivery, seasonal forcing, lake advancement, riverbed epochs and restart recovery still need one coordinated runtime. The independent seasonal audit is opt-in; it does not automatically force weather into the authoritative world.
2. **Cross-region erosion:** Sediment imports/exports must participate in authoritative riverbed terrain commits, with safe drainage rerouting and migration of already-in-transit water/sediment after topology changes.
3. **Distributed safety:** Sender epoch checks and file alias validation are cooperative preflight checks, **not** a distributed lock, authenticated provenance, or globally atomic transaction. Replay history still cannot be safely compacted without a durable global watermark and receiver proof. The epoch rule does not prove that a remote sender has not been maliciously rewritten.
4. **Physical calibration:** Cell areas must be independently authoritative and physically nonoverlapping. The degree-day snow/soil model remains approximate and caller-forced; it does not supply groundwater, calibrated precipitation/temperature/evaporation, timed floods or multiple lake spillways. Planetary catchments, ocean boundaries and large-world fault-injection/endurance tests remain incomplete.

**Safety:** No merge, PR, write to `main` or other branches, production operation or paid infrastructure modification. Continue water/hydrology before other world layers.


## 2026-11-02 — Owned multi-region seasonal runoff and verified frontier routing (CI passed)

**Scope:** Only `Yusuf-cm/wild-deck` on `feature/world-engine-v2`. Starting HEAD `26956b69bb6e6d9cde61f130bfd8e8f5a657f193`; [baseline CI 41391063969](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41391063969) passed **1,050 Maven/JUnit tests**, zero failures/errors/skips, plus world experiment, resource replay, browser-save, atlas export and real-browser checks. No starting failures.

### Implemented

1. **One physical owner per seasonal source across named regions.** New `HydrologySeasonalRegionBudgetV2.advance` preflights all region weather, rejects duplicate seam/pole/boundary samples, missing persisted cells, mixed sampling resolutions, inconsistent season lengths and forged spherical cell areas, and prevents total owned area exceeding the planet. It advances one globally ordered snow/soil checkpoint using the independent SI-volume audit and partitions its runoff by region without reinjecting precipitation. Mixed-LOD area remapping is **not** implemented; mixed resolutions fail closed.
2. **Audited cross-region runoff routing.** `Result.route` routes exactly those runoff sources through the existing global-address flow graph, verifies adjacent canonical edges and explicit outlet ownership, and independently checks that runoff equals verified terminal discharge plus unresolved frontier discharge. Unloaded destinations remain pending, never fake ocean mouths. Known resolved outlet addresses are independent of named region partitions; global mouth discovery is still external.
3. **Seasonal erosion audit integration.** `HydrologySeasonalErosionPreviewV2.calculateFromForcing` now uses `HydrologySnowSoilStateV2.advanceAudited` before seasonal runoff drives erosion/sediment preview. This remains a noncommitting preview.

### Exact verification

- [CI 41396178091](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41396178091) for initial source/test commit `d2d3d9b644f1c4865821bb5d82589d9a3bb5872c`: **1,057 tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`, all other workflow checks passed.
- [CI 41396278627](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41396278627) for routing source commit `e5e0e84a8aa40c97c7ab7919d870448787c54bea`: **passed**.
- [CI 41396282311](https://github.com/Yusuf-cm/wild-deck/actions/runs/41396282311) for final functional test commit `0f9e77e82ae9b6a9f553379d155af1cf865b09b9`: **1,060 Maven/JUnit tests passed**, zero failures/errors/skips, `BUILD SUCCESS`. All **10 new `HydrologySeasonalRegionBudgetV2Test` tests passed**: conservation, deterministic order, shared-vertex ownership, LOD/area/duration rejection, multi-season persisted stores, cross-region seam routing, unloaded frontier conservation and invalid routes. World experiment, resource replay, browser-save, atlas export and real-browser checks passed.
- This final documentation/comment commit triggers its own workflow and must be checked independently.

### Still unfinished: hydrology is NOT complete

1. The seasonal regional coordinator is **opt-in**. It is not wired into automatic authoritative region loading, coupled water/sediment delivery, lake storage, climate forcing, durable riverbed commits or restart recovery. The caller must avoid injecting raw precipitation again.
2. The route graph requires caller-provided terrain edges and verified outlets. It does not discover unloaded terrain, authenticate global ocean mouths, resolve planetary catchments, safely migrate in-transit water/sediment after drainage changes, or remap overlapping mixed-LOD areas.
3. Cross-region sediment imports/exports during terrain epochs, globally atomic commits, durable distributed writer fencing and receipt-history compaction remain unfinished.
4. Degree-day snow/soil weather remains uncalibrated. Multiple lake spillways, groundwater, timed flooding, physically calibrated erosion and planetary endurance/fault-injection tests remain unfinished.

**Safety:** No PR, merge, main/other branch write, production deployment, paid infrastructure or database change. Continue water/hydrology before other world layers.
